In the first probe the animation clock showed `currentTime` 0 at wall +1 s and 1007 ms at +3.5 s, so the second packet (delay 300 + 820 = ends at 1120 ms) had not yet been advanced to its end: it was a frame-starved timeline, not a second sequence. The non-conduit infinite animations that also appear in `getAnimations()` (rail, dock, NORA glyph) are unrelated pre-existing loops.
**Contract:** idle recorded-running = 0 packets ✔; one selection = one bounded sequence (2 packets, the 2 path conduits, ~1.1 s) ✔; focus does not replay from the same click ✔; 0 conduit animations afterwards ✔; no recurrence ✔. - executor-confirmed live: unchanged rule (recorded-active target + Mission-level confirmation), now slimmer/crisper (1.5 px, shorter dash, thinner tail), 3.4 s repeat, max 12.
- blocked/error: never a flowing packet. Selecting such a target runs a one-shot "stall" (amber-grey, travels ~60% and fades, never arrives; kind `stall`). Static: the conduit is a segmented, quieter amber filament plus a square fault seat (matches the square node marker).
- unresolved/ambiguous: no conduit exists (W2); unchanged. Uncertainty language is the marker only: when inspected its outer dashed ring turns once (0.9 s) and settles. No packet, no seat.
**Seats / junctions.** New plan junction kinds `socket` (small ring where a path or recorded-active conduit meets a node: hollow = recorded, filled core = executor-confirmed live, bright on the selected path) and `fault`. Only on path / recorded-active / error conduits (never every edge; cap 60; skipped within 3 px of an existing junction; none at the minimal/phone level). Every seat derives from a real conduit.
**Static emphasis (what reduced motion and phones keep).** A solid bright overlay on executor-confirmed live conduits, a dotted ledger overlay on recorded-running ones, the segmented error filament and all seats. Reduced motion: zero packets, zero repeating animations (measured: 0 running travel/tick/amb animations), node tick animations disabled. Phones (stack): no packets, no seats (minimal level), static node markers unchanged.
**Node-edge response.** Selecting seats the node's leading tick once (0.7 s opacity); an executor-confirmed live node's tick breathes on the live period (opacity .62-1, 3.4 s). Opacity of one pseudo-element only: no layout, filter or shadow. No rAF, no JS timers: all CSS.
**Peek/Workbench & switching.** No new replay path: one-shots are keyed by the existing pulse serial; a Graph<->Pipeline switch plays nothing (measured: 2 selection packets before switching, 0 immediately after and 900 ms after).

**Pins changed (deliberately, per the W5.4B brief).** W3 test said "recorded-running without confirmation is static" and "error conduit: no movement"; the first is replaced by the `recorded` contract (kind, slower than live, <=3, active targets only, no live junction, reduced motion 0); "error: no movement" still holds for repeating/ambient motion and is joined by the stall contract. New group 8: no ticks on done/error missions, stall only on alert conduits and bounded, fault seats only into error nodes, sockets only on path/recorded-active, completed mission at rest has no seats, reduced motion keeps seats and loses only packets, a layout switch never replays a stall.
**Process notes.** CSS Modules hash keyframe names, so my first freeze-frame helper matched nothing (frozen 0); fixed by unanchored matching. A test used `"interrupted"` as a JobStatus (not a member; tsc caught it) - removed. First error render looked like an amber lattice; softened (opacity x.6, dash 6/5) and reduced the one-shot cap. Evidence frames freeze each packet animation at a chosen fraction via the Web Animations API (deterministic), noted in filenames/summary.
**Verification.** tsc clean; eslint clean on the touched TSX/TS and the test; tests: W2 layout, W3 conduits (updated), W4 selection, W5.1, W5.2R geometry, W5.3C2 (others unaffected, ran earlier this session). Browser (1440, 390, reduced-motion emulation) in `growforge-ui/.shots/w54b/`; live uses the labelled `?wtLive` fixture, recorded-running and error use real missions (Lumen & Co; AI Solutions for Global Tech Startups). Not marked approved.

- *Overview.* Recorded state AND recorded progress as two facts (error + 100% stays representable and is stated as not reconciled), timing, questions (n recorded / Not recorded), findings (n parsed, labelled Parsed from text), sources, an Evidence fact, and the recorded Verified flag as its own separate fact; recorded error (sanitised). Evidence: "n sources recorded" / "No source recorded" (alert once the step has finished) / "No source recorded yet" (step not run). - *Sources.* A compact ledger of ALL recorded sources (25 rendered of 25; 0, 1 and 26 tested), `[n]` list position, title (falls back to the recorded URI, never an invented publisher / date / author / credibility), domain link, origin ("Recorded in the dossier" / "on the research step") and a neutral, range-compressed "mentioned in the recorded text of finding 01-05 / all 5 parsed findings" (from W1's marker parse; presence in text, not support). Zero sources: "No source is recorded for this research. The findings, if any, have no recorded evidence behind them."
- *Links.* No existing safe-link helper was found, so one small rule lives in the model (`safeHttpUrl`): only http(s) URLs without embedded credentials become links (`target=_blank rel="noopener noreferrer"`, "opens in a new tab" for screen readers); javascript:, ftp:, credentialed and malformed values are shown as plain text. No link framework added.
- *Output.* The full recorded markdown, unaltered (tested with raw HTML, fences, 25,000 chars): rendered through the existing light formatter that builds React elements only (no innerHTML), so raw HTML is shown as text. Usage reuses the estimate-is-not-a-bill rules (asserted again for a local 0 estimate, a priced model and "Billed cost: Not recorded"). Dependencies: resolved Needs first / Waits on this are selectable (clicking keeps the Workbench open on the new canonical selection with that entity's remembered / default section); an ambiguous reference offers only the explanation entity.
- *Evidence on real missions (no synthetic data used for screenshots).* "AI Solutions for Global Tech Startups": 5 questions, 5 findings, 25 sources, done. **W5.3A - Real Workbench content foundation (2026-10-07 19:10 +06:00, Claude Code). Scope: Mission, Department, Step. Nothing else. W5.2R was not redesigned.**
- *Architecture.* `missionWorkbenchContent.ts` (pure): `workbenchContentOf(worktree, entityId, section)` builds SEMANTIC blocks (facts band, group, relations, text, usage rows, record, step rows, empty, unavailable, note) only from the W1 Worktree + W5.1 capabilities; returns null for anything not covered (the shell keeps its placeholder ONLY for those: Live Research, Team Review, QA, Departments phase, ambiguity). `WorkbenchReading.tsx` + `.module.css` render the blocks (named so to avoid the case-insensitive `MissionWorkbenchContent`/`missionWorkbenchContent` clash). No generic JSON renderer, no API, no schema, no new fetch. `MissionWorkbenchPlane` takes `content` + `onPick`; the plane element is unchanged and still never remounts.
- *Language.* Fact bands (label / value separated by hairlines) -> record groups -> output text -> relationship rows; no cards, tiles, KPI numbers or pills. Provenance only where it changes the reading (e.g. "Planning recommendation", "Not recorded", Parsed/Derived on a fact); never per line.
- *Mission.* Overview: Recorded state, Executor (separate fact), progress, verified flag; shape of the record (phases n of 7, the missing ones named as Not recorded, departments, steps, steps in error with sanitised message + link, unknown-type steps); the full brief. Execution: recorded status + executor confirmation, timestamps / people, recorded active steps (links), the seven canonical phases (selectable when recorded, "Not recorded" otherwise, never waiting / 0%), live notes (labelled bare text), event + revision history shown as Not recorded with W1's reason. Output: final output as formatted text (+ media list). Usage: calls, reported tokens (flagged incomplete), ESTIMATE, BILLED COST "Not recorded", then per-step groups of per-call rows. Approvals / Consultations / Requested changes / Lineage: recorded records only (approval arguments summarised key: value, never raw JSON).
- *Estimate != bill.* `estimateText`: unpriced model -> "No price-table estimate for this model"; usd 0 (local / free models) -> "No per-token price in the table (local or free model)" and NEVER "$0"; priced -> "≈ $x price-table estimate". Mission / step totals say "Estimate", the bill line is always "Billed cost: Not recorded".
- *Recorded-running != confirmed-live.* Overview and Execution show "Recorded" and "Executor" as two facts: confirmed -> "Confirmed live by an executor" (live tone); recorded running + readable executor that does not list it -> "Recorded as running · no executor confirms it" (alert) + a note "not shown as live"; unreadable -> "Executor state could not be read"; not running -> "Not applicable". A recorded-active step says "Nothing here proves a live executor is working on it".
- *Department.* Overview (name, canonical id, origin, steps + state mix, attention rows with sanitised errors, selectable). Assignment: each recorded task in full + activity / step ref (linked only when it resolves to one of this department's unique steps) / route; the specialist sits in a "Planning recommendation" group stating it does not show that anyone executed the work. Steps: EVERY recorded step as a selectable row (label, type, provider, latest activity or error, state, %), scrolls inside the reading plane, never truncated semantically. Dependencies: resolved needs-first / waits-on-this rows (selectable) and an Unresolved group.
- *Step* (also Planning, Client Brief, Final Plan phase nodes; a phase with several steps shows one labelled group per step). Overview: label, activity, type or "Type not recorded" (+ a note naming the recorded kind), department / phase links, state, % , timing, sanitised error. Execution: recorded status, provider, models (from usage records), route, instruction hash and executions, the full sanitised recorded error, and an explicit "Executor: Not recorded" line (no invented executor identity). Dependencies: Needs first / Waits on this (selectable) / Unresolved (plain text; an ambiguous reference offers ONLY the explanation entity, never a candidate). Output: the full recorded output, unaltered, light markdown (headings, lists, fences, tables, bold, code; plain text otherwise), with a character count. Usage: per-call rows (provider · model, tokens in / out or "not reported", duration, time, estimate). Approvals / Consultations: only what W1 attached to this UNIQUE step.
- *Interaction.* Rows and links call the one canonical `select`: Department -> Steps -> a step row keeps the Workbench open, moves selection, attachment, ancestry and Context Thread, and shows the Step's remembered / default section (no close / reopen). New entity / section scrolls the reading plane to the top; after a pick focus lands on the reading plane (the clicked row is gone). Esc -> Peek, Graph <-> Pipeline and section memory unchanged.
- *Honest limits found.* (1) A step whose label only restates its department is shown as "Step N" (W4 rule); in a Department's Steps list each row therefore also shows its latest recorded activity so rows are distinguishable. (2) Real data on this machine has no approvals, consultations, requested changes or lineage, and its models are local (`ollama`), so those sections can only be verified by the contract test, not by screenshots; the mission with 24 duplicated step ids ("AI Solutions...") exercises many-step departments and unique-id handling but its ambiguity sits on Team Review (not built yet), so the unresolved-dependency UI is proved by the test (Final Plan with a duplicated reference), not by a screenshot. (3) Real geometry finding from real content: at 375x667 and 1024x768 the inline sheet left only ~144-194px for reading. Smallest fix, not a redesign: `sheetMin` 280 -> 360 and `sheetMax` 520 -> 560 (the Worktree flow scrolls slightly more); side-by-side geometry unchanged. 1366 reads in a 312px-tall region, 1280 in 264px (internal scroll). (4) The reading column is narrow (~270-310px at 440px plane); long unbroken strings wrap, no horizontal scroll was found.
- *Files.* New: `missionWorkbenchContent.ts`, `WorkbenchReading.tsx`, `WorkbenchReading.module.css`, `scripts/test-dive-mission-worktree-workbench-content.ts`. Edited: `MissionWorkbenchPlane.tsx`, `MissionWorktreeView.tsx`, `missionWorkbenchLayout.ts` (sheet bounds only).
- *Verification.* New contract test (placeholder gone for every visible Mission / Department / Step section while uncovered profiles keep it; sections not in the registry have no content; estimate vs billed incl. the "$0" rule and an unpriced model; recorded-running vs confirmed vs unreadable; approval / consultation binding on unique vs duplicated step ids incl. the Mission-level "Not bound" record; unresolved dependency never a target; department Steps rows == the department's steps and selectable; Workbench stays open on pick; section memory per entity; sanitised errors; full output unaltered; local-model estimate). tsc and touched eslint clean. **W5.1 - Workbench depth and capability contract (2026-10-07 16:30 +06:00, Claude Code). State + registry only; nothing visual. W2, W3, W4 are approved and locked (their old "awaiting review" status lines were updated this session; evidence untouched). Default Missions route untouched.**
- *Depth.* `InspectionDepth = "peek" | "workbench"` (`focus` is W6). It is NOT entity selection: `WorktreeSelection` gained only `depth`, `section` (active Workbench section) and `sectionMemory` (transient per-entity choices, never persisted). Workbench state references the selected entity by its W1 id; there is no second selection, view or NORA selection. Actions: `openWorkbench` (needs an open Peek), `closeWorkbench`, `setSection` (only a section the entity really has, only while open). Esc = Workbench -> Peek (same entity) -> close Peek -> climb (the approved W4 order). Enter on the already-selected node while its Peek is open dispatches `openWorkbench` (only keyboard wiring added; clicks unchanged). A dev-only `?wtDepth` text note and `data-depth` / `data-section` attributes prove transitions; no Workbench geometry.
- *Registry* (`missionWorkbench.ts`): `workbenchCapabilities(worktree, id)` -> `{profile, defaultSection, capabilities[], sections[]}`. Capability state = `available | derived | parsed | recorded-empty | unavailable`, plus `visible`, optional `count` (only where the record gives a number; never a fabricated zero), `reason`, `provenance`, `secondaryRead` (W1 class-B content already on the job; no new fetch). `unavailable` is never visible; `recorded-empty` (a read that succeeded with no rows) is hidden unless the emptiness is itself a fact (research that recorded no sources). Sets: Mission = Overview, Execution, Output, Usage, Approvals, Consultations, Requested changes, Lineage (+ History, always unavailable with W1's reason); Live Research = Overview, Questions, Findings (parsed), Sources, Output, Usage, Dependencies; Department = Overview, Assignment (specialist = planning recommendation, not execution proof), Steps, Dependencies (derived); Step (incl. unknown kind, Planning, Brief, Final) = Overview, Execution, Dependencies, Output, Usage, Approvals, Consultations; Team Review = Overview, Inputs (derived), Review (parsed), Output, Usage; QA = Overview, Inputs, QA (only with the explicit Verdict line, parsed), Output, Usage; Departments phase = Overview, Execution (derived), Dependencies; Ambiguity = Problem (default), References, Candidate identities and nothing else. Only sections whose data is actually recorded are shown (e.g. an ordinary step with no output/usage shows Overview / Execution / Dependencies).
- *Selection change while open.* The Workbench stays open and follows the canonical selection. **W4 - Worktree selection + spatial Peek (2026-10-07 15:48 +06:00, Claude Code). Built on the temporary `?worktree=` route; W4 approved and locked (user; status updated 2026-10-07 16:11 +06:00, Claude Code; evidence below preserved). W5 NOT started. Default Missions route untouched.**
- *Carry-forwards applied.* (1) Rose/pink fault tint retired: errors are amber/neutral in nodes, conduit sheaths, junctions and Peek (the final fault language is still open). (2) Collapsed-department packet gap resolved: a node stands for the drawn entities inside it (W3 `subjectSet`), so selecting a department plays ONE packet along its truthful path in the Pipeline too (the incoming conduits to its steps).
- *Canonical selection model* (`missionWorktreeSelection.ts`, pure reducer): identity = W1 entity id (or `ambiguity:<node id>`); labels and ordinals are never identities. Actions: `select` (a department selection expands it and KEEPS it expanded; a step selection reveals its department), `toggleExpand` (explicit chevron; collapsing the selected step's department falls back to the department), `setView` (view only; clears the pulse so no layout switch ever replays a packet), `pulse` (keyboard-focus feedback), `escape` (closes the Peek, then climbs one level, nothing at mission level), `reconcile` (a stale selection falls back to the nearest valid ancestor from the ancestry captured at selection time, finally the Mission; derived during render, no effect). A typed single-step phase IS its step: a step id resolves to the phase node (Live Research, Team Review, QA, ...). The Lens owns this one state; the header Context Thread, NORA events, ancestry highlight, W3 conduit path and Peek all derive from it.
- *Ancestry* (`missionWorktreeAncestry.ts`, no geometry): Mission > Departments phase > Department > Step; typed phase: Mission > Phase; unknown-kind step: Mission > Step; ambiguity: its node's chain + the marker. Dependencies are separate and never alter ownership ancestry.
- *Peek* (`missionWorktreePeek.ts`, `MissionPeek.tsx`): 2-6 canonical facts (never padded), smoked-glass plane with an optical edge, one attachment filament, no tabs, no backdrop-filter / blur / shadow. Mission: state with the recorded-vs-executor distinction, progress, phases n of 7, departments, steps. Phase: state, progress when recorded, timing, before / after (Derived), error. Live Research: questions, sources + verified / no-source state, error. Team Review: inputs, provider + usage presence, Agreed direction (Parsed from text, only if the markdown has it). QA: Verdict only with the explicit line (Parsed from text). Department: steps + state mix, recorded assignment, attention, specialist labelled as a planning recommendation, not an executor. Step: type (or 'not recorded as a known type'), state, progress, provider / model, dependency counts, sanitized error. Ambiguity: 'Dependency could not be resolved uniquely', reference count, candidate count, no candidate offered.
- *Dependencies.* Resolved prerequisites / dependents are Peek links that select the real target (1 per direction + '+N more'); unresolved references are counted, never linked; ambiguity is its own selectable explanation (marker click or Peek link) and never selects a candidate or expands a department.
- *Placement* (`missionWorktreePeekLayout.ts`): deterministic from layout geometry only (no getBoundingClientRect polling). Desktop: sides right / left / below / above with alignment variants, starting beyond a containing block and its conduit bus; then a free-space scan; single-, two- and three-column variants; if the stage is genuinely full (1366 pipeline) the Peek goes BELOW the content and is scrolled into view once per selection, never over a neighbour. Phones: the stack layout reserves a slot directly under the selected row (everything below moves down, containers grow), so the sheet is in the flow with rail continuity.
- *NORA and header.* Events (`growforge:mission-context`, the Dive detail event) are emitted from the canonical selection (contract unchanged; the legacy emitters are silent on the preview route). Truthful labels: a step with only a generic kind is 'Step N'; a recorded label is used as is. Header (preview route): no eyebrow / big title / purpose line (an sr-only h1 remains); index = recorded count + running / waiting / attention / completed; selected = identity, state, % recorded, counts, Graph / Pipeline control and the Context Thread 'NORA / Mission / Departments / Department / Step' (accessible name "NORA's current working context"; each segment selects that ancestor). The user-facing words 'NORA SCOPE' are gone from the preview route; the shared LensHeader of the other lenses is unchanged (open finding).
- *Keyboard and a11y.* One roving Tab stop (the selected node). Arrows follow the ownership hierarchy (same in both layouts): Up / Down siblings, Home / End, Right deeper (Mission > phase; Departments > department; department > first step; a node with an unresolved dependency > its explanation), Left back (an expanded department collapses first, tree-style), Enter / Space native, Escape closes the Peek then climbs. Nodes keep their DOM identity across layouts, so focus survives Graph <-> Pipeline. aria-labels carry type + label + state; the selected node is aria-describedby its Peek; the Peek is a labelled region; reduced motion: no packets, the Peek resolves by opacity only.
- *Verification.* New `scripts/test-dive-mission-worktree-selection.ts` (ancestry; reducer incl. view switch, expansion, stale fallback, escape; dependency navigation incl. ambiguity; thread + NORA payload equality across layouts; Peek content / provenance; placement over 3 fixtures x 4 stage sizes x both views x every selectable id: inside the stage, never over the selected node or neighbours, phone slot over nothing with 40 px rows; keyboard; reduced motion; no replay on switch). Browser (real missions, bounded ~25 scenarios): 1440 graph / pipeline, 1366, 390, 375; selection preserved across switches both ways with 0 packet animations after a switch; roving stop count 1; focus kept across a view switch; reduced motion 0 animations; Peek never outside the viewport and over no neighbouring node; phones: slot sheets, rows / links / close >= 40 / 44 px, no document overflow. tsc and touched lint clean; W1, W2, W3, W4 and every test-dive-* (28) + surface-location pass; test-dive-missions = 11 PASS then the known Windows EPERM temp cleanup.
- *Open findings.* (1) At 1366x768 pipeline some Peeks (Live Research, Step) sit below the content and scroll into view. (2) Phones show only the last two thread segments (the rest stay in the accessibility tree). (3) The ambiguity marker is visually 20 px with an extended hit area. (4) Semantic NORA actions (`MISSION_ACTION_EVENT`) still address the legacy graph state, not the Worktree selection. **W3 - GrowForge signal conduit system (2026-10-07 14:45 +06:00, Claude Code). Built; W3 approved and locked (user; status updated 2026-10-07 16:24 +06:00, Claude Code; evidence below preserved). W4 NOT started. Still only on the temporary `?worktree=` route.**
- *Files.* `missionWorktreeConduits.ts` (pure plan: segments, junctions, packets, styles), `MissionConduits.tsx` + `.module.css` (SVG renderer), `MissionWorktreeView.tsx` (pulse state, reduced-motion hook, `?wtPlain=1` = the W2 plain connectors kept ONLY as a comparison aid, `?wtLive=1` banner), `useMissionWorktree.ts` (`?wtLive` simulates executor confirmation, test-only, banner says so), `scripts/test-dive-mission-worktree-conduits.ts`. W1 truth and W2 node geometry untouched; the old production Mission canvas and default route untouched.
- *Visual system.* Layers: optical sheath (wide, 3.5-8 px, 4-16% opacity stroke) -> structural filament (0.8-2 px) -> arrowheads (Pipeline dependencies) -> junctions (ring + core, cross ticks when selected/live) -> packets (short stroke-dashoffset dash + a dimmer tail). One cold cyan-white family; error only tints the sheath faintly; differences between relationships are weight / opacity / segmentation / sheath (a style has no colour field). Ownership = quiet 0.8 px filament, no sheath; phase membership = stronger conduit with sheath; department membership = restrained branching conduit with a thin sheath; dependency = primary conduit in the Pipeline, thin dashed secondary line in the Graph. Selection: path conduits brighten and widen, the rest recede to ~50% (never removed). Structure is drawn as ONE path per style class so shared trunks never compound opacity.
- *Pipeline.* Dependency conduits are routed as rounded elbows through shared buses: Research -> one fan-out junction on a single bus -> one branch per department; one reconvergence bus into Team Review; no crossings; data-driven (1..14 departments); expanded steps/long names keep the same buses. Graph keeps W2 geometry; membership conduits are curves from a shared origin.
- *Motion semantics (plan + browser verified).* Selection or keyboard focus: ONE packet per conduit on the ancestry path (ancestors first, 300 ms stagger, 820 ms, iteration-count 1, invisible before and after); re-selection re-keys and replays once; switching layouts never replays; subject with no conduit leading to it plays nothing. Repeating packets ONLY when `mission.execution.liveConfirmed` AND the conduit's target is recorded-ACTIVE (max 12; Mission-level confirmation + recorded-active target, not per-step proof, which does not exist). Recorded running without confirmation, completed, blocked/error, pending: static. Ambiguous dependency: no conduit, packet or junction (the W2 marker remains). Reduced motion: no packets rendered at all (also hidden by CSS), clarity roles only. Phones (stack): level 'minimal' = filament + arrowheads + selected emphasis; no sheath, junction cluster or packets. 1366 (wide): 'light' = thinner sheath.
- *Browser evidence (CDP `document.getAnimations()` on the conduit SVG, real missions).* Step selection: 2 animations (packet + tail), iterations 1, 0 running after 3.5 s; re-click replays 2; Team Review selection: 14 (7 conduits), all finish; idle completed / error / recorded-running-unconfirmed: 0 animations; `?wtLive=1` fixture on the running mission (planning step recorded active): repeating animations only on conduits whose target node is 'active'; reduced motion with selections and with the live fixture: 0 animations; ambiguous mission, Team Review selected: 0.
- *Bounded performance (dev server, 1 cold select + 1 warm interaction at 1440 Pipeline, fresh profile each; W3 vs the W2 plain connectors).* cold longest frame gap 76 ms (W3) vs 90 ms (plain), one >50 ms gap in both (pre-existing lens/mission mount, not introduced by W3); warm 21 ms vs 21 ms, none >50 ms; GPU task max 19/23 ms (W3) vs 18/27 ms; raster ~0; rAF registrations ~288/s vs ~289/s: no new rAF loop, no new hitch, no new GPU stall. No filters/blur/drop-shadow anywhere in the conduit layers.
- *Tests.* New conduit semantics test (idle, selection order/once/replay/reduced/phone, unconfirmed/unreadable/no-executor never live, confirmed-live only active targets and capped, completed/error never, ambiguity, relationship styles without colour, junction topology 1..14 departments, buses, levels). tsc and touched lint clean; W1, W2 layout, conduit tests and every test-dive-* (27 scripts) plus test-surface-location pass; test-dive-missions = 11 PASS then the known Windows EPERM temp-cleanup.
- *Open findings.* (1) Selecting a collapsed department in the Pipeline plays no packet (its conduits attach to the nested step rows once expanded). (2) Error-tinted sheaths on 3-4 of 7 fan branches read faintly pink where they overlap the trunk; tone is per-class so it is easy to tune. (3) Phones have no motion at all by design. (4) The live fixture is a simulation of executor confirmation on a real recorded-active mission; no real mission is currently executor-confirmed.

**W2 - Dual Mission Graph / Pipeline layout engine (2026-10-07 13:58 +06:00, Claude Code). W2 approved and locked (user; status updated 2026-10-07 16:24 +06:00, Claude Code; evidence below preserved).**
- *What.* `missionWorktreeLayout.ts` (pure): `layoutWorktree(worktree, stage, mode, {view, selectedId, expandedDepartmentId})` -> nodes (every node id IS a W1 entity id), gap markers, typed edges, ambiguity markers, `contentHeight`; plus `layoutPair`, `morphFrames` (from/to frame per entity id), `resolveViewState`, `ancestryOf`, `findCollisions`. Deterministic: no measurement, simulation, randomness, timers. Same entity set in both views by construction (visible set depends on worktree + expanded department only).
- *Graph.* The Mission is the anchor; recorded phases orbit it CLOCKWISE in execution order on an ellipse (brief 205deg, planning 238, research 272, [Departments hub 0deg, with the departments in a C-curve fan to its right], team review 52, QA 90, final 128): execution reads as an orbit, not a timeline. Expanded department's steps form a column right of the fan. Dependencies are aggregated to the coarsest visible representative (departments collapse into the Departments hub unless expanded).
- *Pipeline.* Left to right: Client Brief, Planning, Live Research, ONE Departments container (the phase) holding every department block stacked vertically, Team Review, QA, Final Plan. Research fans out to each department (cubic curves), each department reconverges into Team Review. Steps nest inside their expanded department block (membership = containment). The Mission is a header chip above the first columns (so the fan can use the full height). Stack (phones): vertical spine in execution order with a left gutter trunk (fan-out) and right gutter trunk (reconvergence); Graph stack is a drill list with containment rails.

Ambiguous dependency: NEVER a step edge; the affected node gets an amber count marker carrying issues + candidate step/department ids; ONLY when every candidate belongs to ONE department a distinct dotted `ambiguous-dependency` edge (resolution: department-level) is drawn from that department; candidates spanning departments give a marker and no edge. Edges keep W1 relation kinds (ownership, phase-membership, department-membership, dependency) with a `rendering` of line / containment / implicit and a plain-language `meaning`.
- *Temporary visuals (dev only).* `?worktree=graph|pipeline` swaps the selected Mission's canvas for `MissionWorktreeView` (plain boxes/lines, stage-local state, toolbar to switch view). New: MissionWorktreeView.tsx/.module.css, useMissionWorktree.ts (composes the existing /api/jobs/:id, /api/approvals, /api/consultations + executor facts; no new endpoint); one marked block in MissionLens.tsx. Browser matrix on the 4 real missions (job-muj8og2f 7 depts, job-muj7mm3a 8 depts/38 steps/8 ambiguous, job-muf6zmqi 8 depts, job-mumn5hj2 running with Departments missing) x Graph/Pipeline x 1920x1080, 1440x900, 1366x768, 390x844, 375x667 x collapsed/expanded = 70 scenarios: 0 DOM overlaps, 0 horizontal document overflow, selection preserved across switch. tsc/eslint clean; W1, steps, focus, departments, responsive, surface tests pass.

(2) At 375x667 the stage is ~237 px: the stack works but only ~2 rows are visible; it scrolls. (3) Pipeline step chips are 18-24 px high at density (single line). **W1 - canonical MissionWorktree adapter (2026-10-07 13:26 +06:00, Claude Code).**
- *What.* `src/components/spatial/dive/missionWorktree.ts`: pure `buildMissionWorktree({job, approvals?, consultations?, executor?})` -> typed read-only `MissionWorktree` (+ `worktreeQueries()` for owner / phase / department / prerequisites / dependents / feeds / consumers). No fetch, persistence, API, schema, UI, layout or routing change; nothing consumes it yet. Reuses `resolveStepDependencies` (id resolution) and `missionLiveEvidence` (executor truth) rather than re-implementing them.
- *Entities.* Mission, PipelinePhase (7 canonical keys; only RECORDED ones emitted + `missingPhaseKeys`), Department (derived from taxonomy + planSnapshot.assignments + step.departmentId; id `department:JOB/canonical`, mission-scoped), Step (identity `step:JOB/recordedId`, `#ordinal` suffix + `idUnique:false` when a recorded id repeats; ordinal is display only), Output, Source (ALL sources, `index` = the n in [n]), Usage (per call; estimate separate from `billedCost: Unavailable`), Approval, Consultation (attributed to a step only when its recorded id is unique). Relations are typed: ownership, phase-membership, department-membership, dependency (prerequisite -> dependent), recorded-evidence; dependency is never parent/child.
- *Truth rules.* Absent data is `undefined` or an explicit `Unavailable{reason}`, never a default (verified by `"field" in obj` assertions). Provenance per field: classes A/B/C/D internally, user-facing label Recorded / Derived / Parsed from text / Not recorded (no letters for the UI). Recorded status and executor evidence are separate (`MissionExecution.recordedStatus` vs `liveEvidence`/`liveConfirmed`); per-step `executorEvidence` is `Unavailable` and Mission-level confirmation is never extended to steps. No revision entity: `requestedChanges` (job.revisions entries) + `liveNotes` are exposed as recorded; `history.revisionHistory` and `history.eventTimeline` are Unavailable. Research: questions (planSnapshot), findings and [n] markers PARSED from markdown, `claimSourceMapping` Unavailable. Team Review / QA: raw markdown is the Output; `review.sections` and QA `verdict` are Parsed from text, unrecognized headings kept and flagged, no sections fabricated from unstructured text. Departments phase gets `parallel` state derived from the absence of dependencies between department steps (single / parallel / not-parallel). Phases never invent a percent (only single-step phases expose the step's recorded percent).
- *Tests.* `scripts/test-dive-mission-worktree.ts` (10 required scenarios incl. 24-source research, ambiguous/unresolved/self dependency, rerun lineage, missing provider/usage, legacy fixture missing phases, live vs recorded, stable ids, mission-scoped department ids). Passes; tsc and eslint clean; existing steps/focus/departments/surface tests still pass.
- *Real-data run (data/jobs.json projection, read-only).* job-muj8og2f (error, 13 steps): 7 phases, 7 departments, 13 steps, 8 outputs, 0 sources (research errored, no sources), 6 usage, relations dep 18 / own 27 / phase 13 / dept 7, 0 dependency issues. job-muj7mm3a (error): 7 phases, 8 departments, 38 steps, 25 sources, 20 outputs, 18 usage, 32 steps with repeated recorded ids, 8 ambiguous dependency issues (Team Review's references to department ids). job-mumn5hj2 (running, no plan): 6 phases, `departments` MISSING, 0 departments, liveEvidence unconfirmed. job-muf6zmqi (done): 7 phases, 8 departments, 14 steps, 25 sources, 12 usage, 0 issues.
- *Problems W2 must know.* (1) In job-muj7mm3a recorded step ids repeat (8 departments x 4 steps) so Team Review's dependsOn is entirely AMBIGUOUS: its resolved feeds are empty; W2 must draw ambiguity (the issue carries candidate step ids), not guess. (2) A running job can have no department steps/plan yet (Departments phase missing), so the Pipeline layout must tolerate absent phases. (3) A phase can be `error` with a recorded 100% (research with no sources). (4) Local-model usage prices as estimate 0 (price table null = free): it is an estimate, not a billed cost, and must not be shown as 'cost 0'. (5) Department canonical ids differ from step ids (taxonomy canonicalization), so selection must use entity ids, not strings. (6) Research sources and questions need the full Job + dossier/planSnapshot; CoreState alone is not enough.


**W0 - canonical Missions entry routing (2026-10-07 13:18 +06:00, Claude Code).**
- *Data-truth refinements carried forward (user, approved).* (1) A-D provenance is an INTERNAL Worktree-model classification; the UI uses plain language (Recorded / Derived / Parsed from text / Not recorded), never literal A/B/C/D chips; raw classes stay available for audit/debug. (2) No canonical `revision` history sub-record yet: revisions overwrite steps in place, so past revisions are not durably recorded; requested changes may be shown where canonical (job.revisions[] entries, liveNotes), revision history stays `Not recorded` until a later recording phase.
- *Root cause (confirmed in code).* `CoreCommandCenter` Missions HudCard -> `SpatialHud onOpenMissions={() => onSelectTier("core")}` -> `handleSelectTier("core")` = the LEGACY Missions tier (visualMode "missions" + dive to MISSIONS_CAMERA_DIST 90). Explore sets `controls.minDistance = 136`, so the camera is clamped to 136 > the 125 return band in the per-frame resolver, which forces tier "brain"/visualMode "brain" and the surface effect writes `?tier=brain`.
- *Fix (2 source files).* `SpatialCanvas.tsx`: new `openMissionsLens` = `setDiveInitialLens("lens.missions"); startDiveInRef.current()` (the same two calls the popstate/URL path already uses), passed to the HUD as `onOpenMissions`. `SpatialHud.tsx`: new optional `onOpenMissions` prop; the card uses it (fallback to the old tier call only if no opener is supplied). The URL is deliberately NOT pre-armed as a nav target, so the existing surface effect pushes exactly one `?lens=missions` entry when the dive layer is reached. No new navigation mechanism, no change to the legacy overlay, layout, data, NORA, nucleus, Explore.
- *Semantics audit of the other entries (unchanged).* `/core` (`app/core/page.tsx`) redirects to `/?tier=core`; `surfaceLocation` parses `tier=core` as the CORE surface (only `tier=brain` is Explore), so it means CORE and is preserved. `SHOW_CORE` ("CORE Execution", `/?tier=core`, 'core / execution core / command center' phrases) means CORE and is preserved; `SHOW_MISSIONS` already routes to `/?tier=home&lens=missions` (correct). Sidebar/Workspace `href="/core"` are legacy chrome, untouched. Note for the F8/deprecation decision: inside the canvas the string tier "core" still names the legacy Missions overlay; only the Missions card stopped using it.
- *Verification (dev server :3100 via owner-review launcher, headless Chrome 1440x900, history length + URL timeline sampled every 100 ms).* CORE home -> Missions card: URL went `/` -> `/?lens=missions` only (timeline shows no `?tier=core` and no `?tier=brain` at any sample), history 2 -> 3 (one new entry), Dive Missions heading present. Back -> `/`; Forward -> `/?lens=missions` (history unchanged at 3). Dive -> Finance -> Missions lens: `?lens=finance` then `?lens=missions` (one entry each). Refresh on `?lens=missions` and direct `?lens=missions` load: land on the Missions lens. CORE via top nav -> `/`; `/core` -> `/?tier=core` (CORE still reachable). **Mission Worktree architecture contract, approved with three refinements (2026-10-07 13:08 +06:00, Claude Code). No code changed.**
- *Refinement 1: one entity set, two layouts.* `Mission Graph` is the default and primary spatial worktree. `Pipeline` is an ALTERNATE LAYOUT of the same canonical Mission/phase/department/step entities, arranged in execution order (Client Brief -> Planning -> Live Research -> Departments parallel fan-out -> Team Review -> QA -> Final Plan). Switching animates the same nodes between positions. Selection, NORA scope, Workbench state and ancestry survive the switch. Rejected: a horizontal pipeline spine rendered above a second full graph by default (two relationship systems at once).
- *Refinement 2: terminology.* There are 7 canonical pipeline phases/gates (the list above). Departments is the parallel execution phase and contains the canonical department fan-out. The phrase "six stages" is wrong and is not used.
- *Refinement 3: Workbench is spatial, not a sidebar.* Contract: selected graph entity -> attached Peek -> expanding Context/Workbench plane -> Focus reader. The plane may occupy the right-side reading zone at depth but originates visually from the selected entity (grows out of its Peek anchor) and keeps the illuminated ancestry path (Mission -> phase -> department -> step) lit. Focus expands from the Workbench; the graph recedes (dims, stays selectable context) without losing selection.
- *Accepted unchanged.* Canonical real data only; selectable phase nodes; step-id selection (ordinal fallback); real dependency controls; conduit semantics (repeating flow only on executor confirmation, recorded-running static); Peek/Workbench/Focus depth model; reuse of existing Missions V2 architecture; nucleus postponed until layout is stable; roadmap order.

The reference image is composition inspiration only; all content stays canonical (see the audit entry below).


**Mission Worktree read-only audit (2026-10-07 11:35 +06:00, Claude Code). No code changed.**
- *Canonical model (traced).* `Job` in `src/lib/jobStore.ts` (data/jobs.json, max 50) is the only mission record. It holds an ordered flat `steps[]`; each `JobStep` has `kind` (brief|plan|research|department|reconcile|qa|final), `label`, `departmentId`, `runtimeRouteId`, `status`, `percent`, `weight`, `dependsOn[]`, `activity` (free text), `output` (markdown), `media[]` (image|video only), `sources[]` ({title,uri}), `error`, `provider`, `usage[]` (UsageRecord per LLM call: provider, model, in/out tokens nullable, durationMs, timestamp), `instructionsHash`, `instructionExecutions[]` (new jobs), `startedAt`/`finishedAt`. Job-level: brief, title, status (running|done|error), percent, verified, finalOutput, media, error, createdAt/updatedAt/finishedAt, createdBy, approvedAt/By, liveNotes[] (bare strings), revisions[] ({message,createdAt,effect,redoneSteps}), rerunOf, planSnapshot ({title, researchQuestions[], assignments[{departmentId,runtimeRouteId,stepId,task,activity,vaultRecommendation}]}), dossierSnapshot ({text, sources, verified}). Side stores: approvals.json (jobId, stepId, toolName, args, status, createdAt/decidedAt/By), consultations.json (question, options, payload, answer, redirectDirective, answeredBy), telemetryStore (IN-MEMORY, 30 events, global), instruction bundles on disk by hash. The Missions lens reads `/api/core/state` (CoreJobView: per-step outputChars + 700-char preview, token/cost aggregates, departments with task/blueprint, research.sources sliced to 6, finalPreview), `/api/jobs/:id` (the FULL raw Job: used for dependsOn, errors and deep inspection), `/api/approvals` and consultations. `/api/jobs/:id/usage` returns per-call records per step but the Missions UI never calls it.
- *A real pipeline DOES exist.* Fixed canonical order created in `orchestrator.ts`: Client Brief -> Planning (HQ / Executive Orchestration) -> Live Research -> Departments (N parallel steps `dept:<id>`, concurrency 3, each dependsOn research) -> Team Review (reconcile, dependsOn ALL dept steps) -> QA -> Final Plan, with per-step `weight` shares of progress. It is stage-typed by `kind` + `dependsOn`, not user-defined and not invented. No Strategy/Execution/Delivery stages exist.
- *Relationships.* Mission 1-N Steps (flat; no parent/child, no sub-steps). Department is NOT a stored entity: derived from the taxonomy, linked through `planSnapshot.assignments[]` and `step.departmentId` (+ legacy route meta-ads). Stage steps have no departmentId and are NOT graph nodes today. Approvals/consultations key on (jobId, stepId). Reruns create a NEW job (`rerunOf`); revisions reset steps IN PLACE (earlier outputs overwritten).
- *Capability matrix (A readable now / B stored, not exposed by the Missions read model / C partial or ambiguous / D not recorded).* Identity A (rerunOf lineage B). Brief A; objective/instructions C (brief + liveNotes only). Status A (waiting/blocked/unverified are UI-derived). Progress A (step weight B). Departments/assignments A (task text and specialist recommendation are in CoreDeptView but unused by Focus; the recommendation is NOT a dispatch: C). Stages A via kind. Steps/type/dependencies A (raw dependsOn via /api/jobs). Parent/child D. Execution state A (recorded; active is not proof); executor evidence A only job-level (overview snapshot), per-step D. Attempts/retries D (retries only console.warn; revise/resume overwrite; rerun = new job). Model/provider: per step A (research has none), per call B, instructionExecutions requestedModel B (new jobs only). Tool calls D (executed calls not persisted; gated-tool approvals A). Prompts/instructions B (hash + bundles on disk, no API; old jobs only a 12-char legacy hash: C). Inputs D (derivable from dependsOn + outputs, never stored). Outputs A raw (shown only mission-level as plain unrendered text). Files C (image/video MediaItem only). Research queries B (planSnapshot.researchQuestions structured; also inside output markdown). Sources A raw, but only 6 of N reachable in the UI (sample job: 24). Per-question source mapping and citations C (only inline [n] markers inside markdown). Reviews C (Team Review = markdown with prompted headings Conflicts/Dependencies/Gaps/Agreed direction; QA = Verdict + Unsupported claims/Contradictions/Missing essentials/Required fixes; the verdict is also stored as step.activity text; no typed findings; criteria live only in the prompt). Approvals A. Requested changes A (revisions) / liveNotes B (no timestamps). Errors A (strings; classification derived). Logs/telemetry C (in-memory, not per job, not durable; brainLogger overwrites CURRENT_STATE.md and appends DECISION_REGISTRY.md: not a job event log). Token usage A raw / B per call. Cost C (static price-table ESTIMATE; unknown models null; billed cost D). Timestamps A (job, step, call, revision, approval; step created/updated D). History/events C (no event log; job mutated in place). Owners/responsible agents C (createdBy, approvedBy A; per-step actor D). Notes/comments C.
- *What the UI reaches today.* Mission select -> Focus (state, active step, status mix, attention) + Inspect execution = 15 mission-level categories (Identity, Steps, Dependencies, Agents, Context, Tools, Evidence, Cost, Telemetry, Outputs, Files, History, Next steps, Quality, Approvals), the only place full outputs appear. Department -> up to 5 step status rows. Step -> status, percent, 170-char sanitized error, pending-approval/question COUNTS, up to 3 prerequisites and 3 dependents (labels + status only). Deep inspection is NOT reachable from Department or Step. Stage steps (Live Research, Team Review, QA, Plan, Final) are only inert dependency chips (div, tooltip, no handler, not focusable) and non-interactive rows in the Step focus; their output is reachable only via mission-level Outputs/Quality lists. Completed/error steps show no output, sources, usage, provider or timestamps at step level.
- *Live Research can truthfully show (data exists, UI does not surface):* status/percent/activity, started/finished, the research questions (planSnapshot.researchQuestions), per-question findings + answers + [n] markers (output markdown), all sources (title+uri), job-level verified flag, the no-sources error, dependency on Planning and fan-out to every department step; NOT usage/provider (the research call is not recorded in usage in the sample). *Team Review can truthfully show:* status, times, provider, one usage record, the reconcile markdown (Conflicts / Dependencies / Gaps / Agreed direction), its dependsOn = every department step, dependent QA; the decision text is also appended to DECISION_REGISTRY.md.
- *CORE-home MISSIONS card -> old UI -> Explore (cause).* CoreCommandCenter Missions HudCard -> `SpatialHud onOpenMissions -> onSelectTier("core")` -> `SpatialCanvas.handleSelectTier("core")` sets tier core + visualMode missions (the legacy CoreZoomTier overlay fades in) and starts the dive. When the camera lands at `MISSIONS_CAMERA_DIST = 90` (also reduced motion, `?tier=core`, `/core`, and SHOW_CORE in semanticNavigation.ts) OrbitControls clamps it to `controls.minDistance = 136` (set for Explore framing, SpatialCanvas.tsx:577), so d = 136 > the 125 return band in the per-frame tier resolver (`!(currentTier === "core" && d < 125)`), which forces tier brain + visualMode brain, and the surface-location effect writes `?tier=brain`. The legacy surface is therefore structurally unreachable (this is F8's cause). Smallest likely fix: stop routing those entries to the legacy tier: point the Missions card (and SHOW_CORE, /core, ?tier=core where Missions is meant) at the canonical Dive Missions lens (`?lens=missions`, the same `setDiveInitialLens("lens.missions")` + start-dive path the popstate handler already uses). Alternative, not recommended: lower minDistance for the legacy tier. Not changed.
- *Proposed hierarchy.* Mission -> Stage (Planning, Research, Departments group, Team Review, QA, Final; derived strictly from kind+dependsOn) -> Department (assignment + its steps) -> Step (selected by stepId, not department ordinal) -> sub-records: Execution (status/times/provider/usage calls), Evidence (questions, sources), Output (markdown), Review (reconcile/QA sections parsed read-only from the markdown and flagged as parsed), Approval, Consultation, Revision. Depths: PEEK = quick contextual facts beside the graph (status, times, provider, counts, error line, one-line output preview); WORKBENCH = complete structured inspection of the selected entity in a docked panel with tabs per sub-record type (Overview, Input/Dependencies, Execution, Output, Evidence, Usage, Approvals/Questions, History); FOCUS = full reader for one artifact (rendered markdown output, full source list, usage table, revision/approval history) while the graph stays dimly visible.
- *Recommended sequence.* 1) fix the Missions entry routing (tiny, isolated); 2) a read-only MissionWorktree adapter assembling existing sources (full /api/jobs/:id + /usage + approvals + consultations + planSnapshot + dossier) into typed Stage/Step/Evidence/Usage entities (no schema change, no new endpoint needed initially); 3) make stage steps first-class selectable entities and the dependency chips real controls; 4) Peek, then Workbench, then the Focus reader (markdown rendering, all sources, usage table) with every field labelled by provenance class; 5) only then a SEPARATE approved recording phase for what is not recorded (attempt history, tool-call receipts, stored inputs, structured review verdicts/findings, durable event log, per-step actor, file artifacts with metadata); 6) holographic pipeline design last, on the real stage model above.

**Process note.** The first 15C pass ran two long unattended batches (~30 and ~19 Chrome launches) and waited on them with 5-minute sleeps; the user stopped it. Both batches actually completed (no background task failed; earlier failures were my own script bugs fixed in the foreground: a template-literal syntax error, `Math.max(...hugeArray)` stack overflow, a heavy per-frame sampler that itself cost ~240 ms/1.4 s, and a MutationObserver that never recorded). **Valid measurements (rAF frame-gap sampler = timestamps only, so it does not perturb; mission = first Mission Field stratum; `next dev`, i.e. React DEV build).** Real GPU confirmed by CDP `SystemInfo.getInfo`: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5050 via ANGLE/D3D11, gpu_compositing + rasterization + webgl `enabled`. The software mode the earlier finding came from reports `Microsoft Basic Render Driver`, gpu_compositing/rasterization/webgl `disabled_software`.
| Environment | Viewport | Motion | Visible stall | Longest frame gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GPU headless, cold shader cache, FIRST selection | 1920/1440/390 | normal | yes | 445 / 417 / 570 ms |
| same | 1920/1440/390 | reduced | yes | 438 / 486 / 326 ms |
| GPU, later selections in the same browser | 1920/1440/390 | normal | small hitch | 104 / 90 / 69 ms |
| same | 1920/1440/390 | reduced | minor | 70 / 76 / 49 ms |
| GPU, disk cache warm, 1st select of a fresh launch | 1920 / 1440 / 390 | normal | yes at 1920, mild else | 347 (1 of 2) / 132 / 104 ms |
| **HEADED real window (RTX 5050)**, first selection | 1440 | normal / reduced | yes | 417, 299 / 604 ms |
| HEADED, later selections | 1440 | normal / reduced | small hitch | 83-118 / 56-62 ms |
| Software (`--disable-gpu`), all selections | 1920/1440/390 | both | jank throughout (20-29 frames >50 ms in 2.9 s) | 133-267 / 133-183 / 50-83 ms |
**Reproduces on real hardware: YES.** The 300-450 ms stall is NOT only a software-rendering artifact: it appears (and is larger than in software) on the first mission selection after a browser/GPU-process start, in both headless-GPU and headed Chrome on the RTX 5050, with and without reduced motion. It disappears after that first selection (later selections: 50-120 ms single-frame hitch). Software rendering is a separate, different problem (slow throughout).
**Diagnosis (shortest defensible).** (1) COLD hitch = one-time GPU first-use cost: the trace shows a single ~200-245 ms task on the GPU main thread inside `RendererRasterWorker -> RasterDecoderImpl::DoEndRasterCHROMIUM::Flush` (renderer main and compositor idle), i.e. Skia GPU shader/pipeline compilation on the first raster of the nucleus layers. One-variable isolation (CSS injected before the click, fresh profile each launch, 1440x900 normal, cold first-select gap): baseline 431/410/403 ms; all CSS `filter` off 222/229; nucleus-only fx (filter+shadow+mask) off 250/264; Field-only fx off 479/389 (no effect); masks off 417/417; shadows off 396/417; backdrop off 347/403; animations off 514/486; nucleus gradients off 347/368. So about 160-190 ms of the ~410 ms is the nucleus's two CSS filters (`.svg` drop-shadow(0 14px 22px), `.seat` blur(10px)); GPU max task 198 ms -> 115 ms without filters. The remaining ~220 ms is not isolated (candidates: other first-use GPU programs, cold V8). (2) WARM hitch (~85-120 ms at ~+390-450 ms, normal motion) is MAIN-THREAD, not GPU: GPU tasks max 4 ms; one React scheduler task `performWorkUntilDeadline` 60-85 ms (DEV React: `jsxDEV`/`runWithFiberInDEV` dominate the profile) followed by `MissionDepartments.useLayoutEffect.measure` forcing a 22-25 ms style/layout recalc (1486 elements); CSS variants do not change it (76-118 ms everywhere). Duplication check: others are at effective opacity ~0 by ~+245 ms while the Field lingers inert until ~+520-540 ms; opacity-0 strata are not a measured cost (GPU idle in the warm case); no fully-painted duplicate heavy surface found.
**Decision gate.** Outcome C for the cold stall's remaining ~220 ms; partially isolated (filters ~160-190 ms) but no change made: a permanent fix (e.g. pre-warming the nucleus filters off-screen at lens mount, or replacing the drop-shadow/blur) is a material/architecture decision and the user forbade material changes before proof. The warm hitch is dev-build React work and needs a production-build comparison before any change. **No product code changed; no files changed besides this entry.**
**Manual check (bounded, 2 minutes, normal Chrome with hardware acceleration).** 1) `chrome://gpu` -> confirm Graphics Feature Status shows `Hardware accelerated` for Canvas/Compositing/Rasterization/WebGL. 2) Fully quit Chrome, reopen, open the app, Dive In -> Missions at your usual window size. 3) DevTools -> Performance -> Record, click the first mission, stop after ~2 s: look for the longest frame (red/yellow bar >50 ms) at ~+400-650 ms and, in the GPU lane, one long task. 4) Reselect (All missions -> another mission) and record again: the long one should be gone, leaving at most a ~100 ms main-thread hitch. 5) Optional: `npx next build` then `next start` to see whether the warm hitch survives without React dev mode.

**15B2 LOCK (user, 2026-10-07).** Locked strategy: hidden record-label layer = zero recurring SpatialCanvas geometry reads; container size cached + invalidated by the window-resize handler and a container ResizeObserver; per-label size read once per frame while labels are genuinely visible; transform-moving panel bounds are NOT cached via ResizeObserver; pointer bounds stay event-driven; rAF topology unchanged. F9 (CoreZoomTier.computeFlow periodic reads from the hidden legacy overlay) and F10 (Explore-visible label placement geometry work; needs a separately approved batched measure/layout pass) stay PARKED and must not be optimized in 15C.
**Root cause (instrumented headless Chrome, 1920x1080; Element.getBoundingClientRect + clientWidth/clientHeight + offsetWidth/offsetHeight wrapped, attributed by stack; rAF callbacks identified by signature; Performance.getMetrics for LayoutCount/Duration).** The record-label block of `SpatialCanvas`'s single rAF `animate` loop did ALL of its geometry work every frame even when the label layer is hidden (everything outside Explore): `container.getBoundingClientRect()` (1), `aside, nav[aria-label="Primary navigation"]` rects (3: two navs + the Explore index panel), and `projectToPixels(ZERO_VEC)` / core-edge (container.clientWidth/clientHeight, 3+3) = ~10 synchronous reads/frame (~600/s at 60fps; the original ~700/s finding). Those values feed ONLY label placement (`occupied`, `corePx`, `coreRadius`), and the per-label `px` is `null` unless `visible > 0.01`, so with labels hidden none of it could ever be used. Reads were identical every frame (same-geometry ratio ~78%).
**Call-site inventory (SpatialCanvas).** Per-frame (animate): label block container rect + panel rects + reader rect, `projectToPixels` clientWidth/Height, per-visible-label `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight` (12 reads per label per frame: 3 candidate expressions x2 + the box map x2, repeated). Event-driven: `coreScreenHit` (pointerdown), pointermove NDC rect (1 read per move event), pointerdown/up rects, the window resize handler (clientWidth/Height once per resize). Not SpatialCanvas: `CoreZoomTier.computeFlow` rects (~10 reads/s on a 1s timer while a job is `running`, in the hidden legacy CORE overlay) and `measureEnvironment`/Mission list rects (dive, only during transitions).
**Invalidation model chosen.** (a) panel/viewport/core geometry: not needed at all unless the label layer is live (`labelsLive = visible > 0.01`), so it is measured only then, as before (no behavioural change when labels are shown); (b) container size: effectively static until a window/container resize, so cached in `containerSize`, refreshed by the existing window-resize handler (same tick as the renderer resize, so no stale frame) plus one container-level ResizeObserver (disconnects on cleanup); (c) per-label size: measured once per label per frame into locals (nothing writes to the label between the old reads, so values are identical). Rejected: caching the panel rects with ResizeObserver/transition invalidation (panels slide by transform, which ResizeObserver does not report, so it risks stale occupancy for a visible-labels-only gain); a MutationObserver on body; any new global geometry state.
**Implementation (`SpatialCanvas.tsx` only).** `containerSize` cache + `containerSizeObserver` (declared before `handleResize`, updated in it, disconnected in cleanup); `projectToPixels` and the label bounds test use it; `labelsLive` gate around the occupied-rect reads and around `corePx`/`coreEdge`; `labelW/labelH` hoisted. Same rAF loop (rAF count unchanged: it only removes work inside the existing loop). New `scripts/test-spatial-layout-reads.ts` (4 source-guard checks).
**Before -> after (reads per 4s steady window unless marked burst; forced-layout proxy = browser LayoutCount in the same window).**
| State | Before reads | After reads | LayoutCount before -> after |
|---|---|---|---|
| CORE idle | 1110 | 20 | 108 -> 121 |
| Overview idle | 1340 | 40 | 4 -> 4 |
| Mission Field idle | 1330 | 20 | 4 -> 4 |
| Selected Mission idle | 1063 | 40 | 4 -> 4 |
| Departments idle | 2410 | 20 | 4 -> 4 |
| Explore idle (labels live) | 17517 | 2348 | 996 -> 1094 |
Bursts (1.5s windows): select mission 324 -> 56; Selected -> Overview 303 -> 6; Dive lens switch 895 -> 25; return Dive -> CORE 191 -> 1. Pointer: 60 moves over CORE 1830 -> 80 (1 read per move event remains, event-driven); 60 moves in Explore 48522 -> 5923. Resize 1920 -> 1440 -> 1366 -> 390 -> 1920: burst reads 323/443/602/364 -> 35/35/34/36 (the window handler's reads + the CoreZoomTier ticks), idle after each 630-1040 -> 20 (one idle window 0), after 6 rapid resizes 700 -> 20; no polling continues after a resize. rAF: loops 1-3 and callback counts the same before/after in every state (the optimization changes no loop count). Scripting time over the same windows is lower but noisy (CORE idle 382 -> 290 ms/4s, Departments 482 -> 362, Explore 1558 -> 1243); layout duration was already ~1-15 ms/4s so no reliable layout-time claim. LayoutCount is unchanged (CORE idle ~1 layout/frame both before and after: it is caused by per-frame DOM style writes elsewhere in CORE home, not by the removed reads, and costs ~15 ms/4s).
**Remaining reads, explained.** (1) ~10/s in CORE/Overview/Departments idle: `CoreZoomTier.computeFlow` (1s timer, hidden legacy overlay, F8 territory; NOT SpatialCanvas; left untouched per instructions). (2) 1 gBCR per pointermove (raycast NDC): event-driven, a real interaction. (3) Explore with labels live: ~590/s = 2 offset reads x ~30 labels + the panel rects per frame (was ~4400/s); these genuinely depend on per-frame font size/transform and animated panels, so they stay per-frame while labels are live. A deeper batch (write all label font sizes, then read all sizes) is possible but changes the write/read order and was left out of this bounded pass.
**Regression evidence.** Explore label counts before/after (same state): 25 vs 24-25 visible at 1920, 3 at 390, 25 after returning to 1920 (container size cache followed the resize correctly both ways); no console/exception errors in any run; CORE/NORA/Dive/Missions code untouched. Nothing was changed in layout, CSS or rendering order; label placement logic is byte-for-byte the same when labels are live apart from reading cached/hoisted identical values.
**Tests.** `tsc --noEmit -p .` clean; eslint clean on `src/components/spatial/dive`, `SpatialCanvas.tsx` and the new script; `test-spatial-layout-reads` 4 checks; all 24 `test-dive-*` + `test-surface-location` pass; `test-dive-missions` passes its assertions then the known Windows EPERM.

## 2026-10-07 00:42 +06:00 (Claude Code) - (superseded by the 15B2 entry above) PHASE 15B1 (CoreSphere3D inactive lifecycle) APPROVED AND LOCKED (user). UNCOMMITTED. 15B2 (SpatialCanvas getBoundingClientRect) NOT started. Phases 0-14 + 15A locked

**15B1 LOCK (user, 2026-10-07).** Locked: visual rendering inactive unless the legacy CORE overlay is visibly active; GL/scene stay mounted; resume continues with no duplicate loops; CORE jobs/app/backend state fully independent of renderer activity; an inactive resize may repaint one frame without restarting the loop. F8 stays PARKED (legacy CORE pipeline/CoreSphere3D surface unreachable as a settled visible surface; `/?tier=core` and the CORE-home Missions entry resolve to Explore/brain): no routing fix, no deletion, no product decision in 15B2. NEXT: 15B2 (SpatialCanvas layout reads).
**Root cause (measured; headless Chrome CDP at 1440x900 with an instrumented requestAnimationFrame wrapper that identifies the CoreSphere3D `animate` callback, tracks pending chains by id, and counts WebGL draw calls per canvas).** `CoreSphere3D` (components/core, a SEPARATE three.js renderer, ~37 draw calls per frame) lives inside `CoreZoomTier`, the legacy "CORE pipeline tier" overlay that `SpatialCanvas` ALWAYS keeps mounted (`opacity-0 inert` unless `currentTier==="core" && visualMode==="missions"`). Its rAF loop had no off-switch, so it rendered every frame in EVERY state, including plain CORE home (not just behind the Dive): CORE home, Overview, Missions, Departments, every return. Exactly one loop (no duplicates across navigation cycles), so it is wasted work, not a leak.
**Before (per 4s phase, 2 full navigation cycles; calls = CoreSphere3D render callbacks, draws = its GL draw calls; counts are deterministic, not vsync-dependent):** CORE home 112 calls / 4144 draws; Overview 96-115 / 3552-4255; Missions 136-171 / 5032-6327; back to CORE 78-87 / 2886-3219; Departments 169-171 / 6253-6327; pending loops always 1.
**Change.** `CoreSphere3D` gets an `active` prop (default true, so any other caller is unchanged). Inside the existing effect the loop is wrapped in `run`/`halt` (exposed through a ref); an `[active]` effect calls them. The scene, GL context and canvas are never torn down; the animation clock is shifted by the paused duration on resume so every time-based term continues instead of jumping (rotation is accumulated per frame and is untouched); a resize while paused repaints one frame (resizing clears the drawing buffer); a sphere mounted inactive paints one frame so it is never blank when it appears. `CoreZoomTier` takes `active` (default true) and forwards it ONLY to the sphere; its own `/api/core/state` polling and job state are untouched. `SpatialCanvas` passes the signal it ALREADY uses for that overlay's visibility: `active={currentTier === "core" && visualMode === "missions" && !diveLayer}`, so no new navigation state. Only visual rendering is gated; jobs, NORA, orchestration, telemetry and the CORE backend are not involved.
**After (same harness, same phases).** CoreSphere3D: 0 calls / 0 draws in ALL phases (CORE home, Overview, Missions, Departments, every return), pending loops 0, canvas still mounted. Everything else renders as before (other draws/frame counts within normal run-to-run variance: e.g. CORE home 560 -> 590, Missions 155-193 -> 196-207).
**Resume / regression evidence.** The legacy tier can't be held visible through the real UI in this build (`/?tier=core` and the CORE-home "Missions" card both end on Explore, `?tier=brain`; logged as F8 below), so I verified visible<->hidden with a TEMPORARY route (`src/app/p15b-harness/page.tsx`, deleted after the run) rendering the real component with a toggled `active`: active 180 calls/3s, 1 pending loop; paused 0 calls / 0 draws over 2.5s; resumed ~150 calls / 5550 draws per 2.5s; 5 pause/resume cycles: pending loops 1 after every resume (no duplicate loops), cancels balance. Canvas pixels (screenshot, sphere region lit-pixel count): active 93191, paused 93929 (stays painted), 120ms after resume 94695 (no blank/dark frame), paused + window resized 84924 (repainted, not blank). In the real app the Missions-card click transiently activates the sphere (26-32 calls) and then halts again in all 3 cycles, pending loops 0 after (regs/cancels balanced), i.e. activation/halt through the real signal works. Console/exception errors: none in any run. NORA/Dive/Missions: untouched files; their draw activity unchanged. Reduced motion: CoreSphere3D has no reduced-motion handling (unchanged; it now simply renders nothing while hidden).
**Tests.** `tsc --noEmit -p .` clean; eslint clean on `src/components/spatial/dive`, CoreSphere3D, CoreZoomTier; all 24 `test-dive-*` + `test-surface-location` pass; `test-dive-missions` passes its assertions then the known Windows EPERM (unchanged).
**Files changed:** `growforge-ui/src/components/core/CoreSphere3D.tsx`, `growforge-ui/src/components/spatial/CoreZoomTier.tsx`, `growforge-ui/src/components/spatial/SpatialCanvas.tsx` (ONE line: the `active` prop on `<CoreZoomTier>`; the getBoundingClientRect loop is NOT touched), `state.md`. Wired by a `ref` on the field div in `DepartmentsLens.tsx` / `AgentsLens.tsx`. Chosen over alternatives: (a) separate magic top values per viewport (rejected: breaks the moment the scope chip or purpose wraps differently), (b) changing LensHeader/shared geometry (out of scope, locked chrome), (c) CSS-only: impossible, the header height is content-driven. Breakpoint is 600px, NOT the 900px phone-chrome query, so tablets (601-900) and desktop keep the exact pre-existing rules.

Not a code bug: re-verified in headless Chrome (CDP script in the session scratchpad `p15a.mjs`), where the value is correct (245px). Do not trust ResizeObserver-dependent measurements in a hidden pane.

## 2026-10-07 01:20 +06:00 (Claude Code) - Phase 14 REVISION (user review): 3 transition bugs fixed, lens rail unified on every lens, relationship motion no longer implies live execution. UNCOMMITTED; Phase 14 still NOT approved by me; Phase 15 NOT started

**User-approved in Phase 14:** material treatment, Mission Field motion, responsive convergence fix, Focus materialization, mobile restraint, reduced motion, performance approach, the pause of dissolved Overview animations. **One semantic change was required before lock (below).** Pre-existing items LOGGED for Phase 15, deliberately NOT fixed: (1) `SpatialCanvas.useEffect.animate` calls `getBoundingClientRect()` ~700x/s at idle while the Dive is open; (2) `CoreSphere3D`'s rAF loop keeps running behind the Dive.
**1. Relationship motion must not imply live execution (`missionEdgeSemantics.ts` `edgeFlow` / `travelMode`; graph + wide traces).** Exact behaviour of a lit (in-focus) edge: NON-LIVE (every edge today, incl. recorded-running without executor evidence, completed, failed, interrupted, blocked, waiting, unverified): ONE parent->child light traversal when the edge becomes lit (hover, keyboard focus, selection change): 2.1s (graph) / 2.2s (wide), cubic-bezier(.45,.05,.3,1), iteration 1, fill both (rests invisible at the end); afterwards the edge stays brighter/thicker but STATIC; moving the focus away and back replays it once. LIVE = `data-flow="live"` only when (a) nucleus evidence is `confirmed` (an executor confirms the mission is running; `MissionSummary.live`, the single source: `presentation.evidence === "confirmed"`) AND (b) the edge leads to something recorded as active right now (a department with an active step, or that active step); only then the lit edge repeats (5.2s / 5.6s loop, travelling the first ~52%, resting the rest) and keeps repeating while it is lit. Unfocused edges never carry light. Idle non-directional breathing of the soft underlay is unchanged (a few percent, 19-31s, no direction). Dependency edges stay dashed/neutral and get no travel; attention rings stay static. Verified live on the real missions: no `data-flow="live"` anywhere; recorded-running mission evidence = "unverifiable"; lit edges on blocked / completed / unverified missions run `edgeTravelOnce` iterations=1, no infinite travel.
**Blocked nucleus.** The irregular rim flicker was reduced so it cannot read as activity: opacity only, ~5% (.83-.88, was .76-.90), TWO brief uneven dips per 29s (was ~9 events per 9.7s), nothing moves, no colour. (The idle rim-light drift `arcDrift`, a slow non-directional 11s breath, is unchanged.)
**Tests.** `test-dive-mission-motion.ts` is now 14 checks, including: policy truth table (`edgeFlow(false, true)` and every non-lit emphasis => no repeating light); every mission state's nucleus evidence is never `confirmed` without an executor snapshot; CSS proof that EVERY repeating `.travel` rule requires `[data-flow="live"]` and `[data-emphasis="lit"]` and every other travel rule runs exactly once (1 both); `data-flow` is only ever produced through `edgeFlow()` and traces only read the row's flag; dependency edges/attention rings carry no travel; blocked-disturbance amplitude/period/irregularity.
**2. Missions -> Overview was a cut, not a fade (root cause).** A higher-specificity shell rule (`[data-dive-journey] .shell > :not(.interior...) { opacity: var(--dive-content-opacity, 1) }`) overrode `.missionLayer[data-leaving]{opacity:0}`, so the Mission layer held at opacity 1 and vanished when it unmounted (~440ms). The leaving opacity now wins (`!important`), eased `cubic-bezier(.4,0,.6,1)` .38s. Measured per frame: Mission layer 1 -> .84 (146ms) -> .39 (261ms) -> .04 (372ms) while the Overview cards return (.09 at 261ms, .51 at 372ms, .8 at 482ms) and NORA un-recedes (.58 -> .96): a crossfade like the way in.
**3. On selection only the strata above and below the chosen one lingered.** The hover rules that soften the hovered stratum's direct neighbours (`!important`) kept winning during the pick. `.field[data-picking] .item .stratum:not([data-picked])` now forces opacity 0 (`!important`, higher specificity). Measured: every other stratum ~.4 at 114ms and .03 at 239ms, neighbours included.
**4. Selected mission -> All missions was not as smooth as the way in (root causes).** (a) The nucleus anchor's release used a transition, but its entrance animation (`fill: both`) pinned opacity at 1, so it popped out when it unmounted; it now uses a release keyframe (`nucleusRelease`, .24s cubic-bezier(.4,0,.6,1): opacity 1 -> 0 + scale .95). (b) After the release there was an empty beat (~200ms) before the strata began; the Field now MOUNTS while the nucleus is still releasing (`returning`), with no entrance delay, a tighter 24ms stagger and a .5s unfold (was .64s + .2s delay). The shared dismiss timer is 240ms (was 180ms). Measured per frame: nucleus 1 -> .96 (82ms) -> .7 (137ms) -> .42 (179ms) -> .1 (261ms) -> gone; strata 0 -> .09 (137ms) -> .28 (261ms) -> .7 (369ms) -> .9 (505ms). Reduced motion: immediate.
**5. Lens rail consistent on every lens.** The Overview rail's black glass, size and position were scoped to `[data-lens="Overview"]`, the other lenses used older rules, so size/position/background changed between lenses. Now ONE rail on all lenses: desktop = the Overview glass (3px padding, 11.5px labels, pill, bottom 49px, the slow top-edge reflection), phone = the compact 46px scrollable rail at bottom 56px; the phone composer / global-nav compaction (Phase 13) now applies to every Dive lens so the three persistent surfaces are identical everywhere (this OVERRIDES the earlier "Missions only" decision at the user's request; the Overview phone content area height was re-based to `calc(100dvh - 240px)`). The rail eases padding/border/shadow over .32s. Measured at 1920, 1440 and 390 on all 9 lenses: rail rect, padding, border, radius, glass and button height identical (1 distinct signature), composer top identical; no horizontal overflow; no actionable control overlapped by the composer on any phone lens (Finance's clipped "Inspect" rows sit inside its own scroll grid). The Missions-only rail reflection was removed (the unified rail carries it).
**Files this revision.** `DiveOverview.module.css`, `DiveOverview.tsx` (dismiss timer 240ms), `MissionsFoundation.module.css`, `MissionField.tsx/.module.css`, `MissionLens.tsx`, `MissionDepartments.tsx/.module.css`, `MissionFlow.tsx/.module.css`, `MissionNucleus.module.css`, `missionEdgeSemantics.ts`, `src/app/globals.css`, `scripts/test-dive-mission-motion.ts`, `scripts/test-dive-mission-responsive.ts`.

**What changed.** New `dive/MissionsAtmosphere.tsx` + `MissionsAtmosphere.module.css`, mounted by a 2-line edit in `DiveOverview.tsx` only when `lens === 'Missions'` (sits directly after `DiveInterior`, under all content; `aria-hidden`, `pointer-events:none`). Layers: opaque obsidian base; stacked low-alpha teal radial falloff (core concentration at 50%/46% where the nucleus will live, wider mid/outer falloff, lower-left and top-right depth planes); static SVG with 8 long curved traces radiating from (960,500) masked by a radial fade, plus two huge faint ellipse arcs as depth planes; two dust SVG layers (64 far / 26 near points, deterministic seeded generator so SSR == client, centre thinned) with compositor-only translate drifts (150s / 96s); one tiny feTurbulence data-URI grain at 5% (dithers banding in the dark gradients); edge vignette. No grid, cards, nodes, metrics or text.
**Tried / rejected.** First pass (iter1) had the right structure but the teal falloff was too faint to read as atmosphere against the near-black; raised glow alphas ~1.5x and strengthened the lower-left plane (iter2 = final). Rejected: reviving THREE `MissionField` (dead code, second scene), a canvas dust field (static SVG + 2 CSS drifts is enough), and restyling `.interior` (would leak into Overview).
**Performance.** No JS timers/rAF/state; `contain:strict`; animations are transform-only on two layers, `will-change` only on those; mobile hides the near-dust layer and softens traces; `prefers-reduced-motion` stops all motion. 3 SVGs, 76 circles total, one 160px grain tile.
**Verified.** tsc clean; eslint clean on both files; test-dive-foundation, test-dive-overview, test-dive-journey, test-overview-briefing, test-overview-snapshot, test-surface-location, test-dive-inner-core pass; test-dive-missions: all 11 assertions PASS, exits 1 only on its known Windows temp-dir rmSync EPERM. Live on :3100 (owner-review): atmosphere is 1440x900 / 1920x1080 exactly, pointer-events none, aria-hidden, no horizontal overflow, 16 mission rows still present, 0 console warnings/errors; `?lens=overview` renders with NO atmosphere element (Overview untouched). Not verified: real frame rate (sandbox browser is slow), 390px mobile screenshot, interactive hover states. Shots `growforge-ui/.shots/M1-before-1920.png`, `M1-after-1920.png`, `M1-after-1440.png` (+ iter1/iter2).
